# VB6 Language Surface — What HexIDE Runs

> **Scope.** Every built-in of the VB6 *language*: statements, functions, operators, keywords and
> modifiers, literals and type names, compiler directives, intrinsic constants, and the in-box objects a
> program can touch without adding a reference. **1184 names**, each with HexIDE's current support level.
>
> **Sibling docs.** This is the *positive* inventory — what runs. The *gap catalogue*, which classifies
> why something is missing (Deferred / Walled / Platform / Partial), is
> [`interpreter-gaps.md`](interpreter-gaps.md); where they disagree, that one is authoritative on
> classification and this one on coverage. Verified runtime *semantics* live in
> [`vb6-fidelity-oracle/README.md`](vb6-fidelity-oracle/README.md). Debugger divergences are in
> [`debugger-vb6-divergences.md`](debugger-vb6-divergences.md). Permanently excluded features are in
> [`OUT_OF_SCOPE.md`](OUT_OF_SCOPE.md), and the IDE surface (menus, windows, designer) is
> [`MISSING_FEATURES.md`](MISSING_FEATURES.md).
>
> **Living document.** Update a row the moment its status changes.
> Diagnostic wording below reflects #935: missing-member messages omit receiver types, and value text
> prints inner type names without Either wrappers. This wording is HexIDE's, not a VB6 measurement.

## How to read this

The statuses are ordered by what a user experiences when they press **F5**, because that — not semantic
fidelity — is what decides whether someone keeps the IDE open. A missing statement is a cliff; a rounding
divergence is a footnote.

| Status | What happens on F5 | Why it ranks here |
|---|---|---|
| **Silently wrong** | Runs, does nothing or the wrong thing, program continues | **Worst of all.** Nothing announces it, so the user debugs their own correct code. |
| **Won't load** | The module never opens | Nothing runs, and the user cannot even look around. |
| **Dies** | Parses, then stops partway through with an error the user sees | The program gets going and falls off a cliff — but it *tells you*. |
| Partial | Runs, with a real restriction | Fine *if the restriction is written down* — which is what the Detail column is for. |
| No-op | Accepted and deliberately ignored | Acceptable only where ignoring it cannot mislead. See below. |
| Supported | Works for the ordinary case | |

**Why "Silently wrong" outranks "Won't load".** A file that will not open is obvious in a second, and the
user knows to blame the tool. A `Shell` that launches nothing, or a `Time = Now` that silently creates a
variable called `Time`, sends someone hunting through their own logic for a bug that is ours. That is the
same harm shape as a silently lossy save, which
[`serialization-outcomes.md`](serialization-outcomes.md) rules out entirely.

**The mechanism, because it generalises.** An unregistered bare name is not an error in VB6's default
configuration and was not one here either: it fell through to implicit declaration and evaluated to
`Empty`, so `s = CurDir` yielded Empty rather than raising, with only the `Option Explicit` path — **off
by default**, as it was in VB6 — turning it into an error. Every intrinsic *called bare*, without
parentheses, was therefore a candidate for this category rather than for Dies.

**That mechanism is closed** as of [#191](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/191): both the read
path and the bare-statement path now raise for any name VB6 defines as an intrinsic, keyed off a list of
VB6's *surface* rather than of our gaps — so implementing a function needs no edit to it, and a name we
have never heard of still gets VB6's own "Sub or Function not defined".

The category was kept at zero rather than deleted, because the shape is what matters: **anything that
fails without saying so belongs here rather than under Dies.** It did not stay at zero. Assignment through
a type character (`s$ = "x"`) was found doing exactly that by a different mechanism, and the six type
characters sat in it until #739 fixed assignment targets. Reads through them run too since #742, so all six are now Partial.

**Until [#754](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/754), most Dies rows told only the test suite.**
The classification above was made by running constructs headlessly, where the exception reaches the test.
In a running IDE, how a row dies decides whether the user hears about it:

- **By `NotImplementedException`, which is most of them, or by any other .NET exception.** A form's event
  procedures and `Sub Main` both stopped at a catch that only logged, so a click handler ended part way
  while the program carried on, and F5 on a `Sub Main` project opened nothing. Until #754 these rows were,
  at the keyboard, Silently wrong. Now the run ends and the runtime-error dialog says what HexIDE does not
  implement, or reports VB6's Internal error (51) with the .NET exception for the rest. Neither is
  trappable, because `On Error Resume Next` stepping over a statement that never ran would be a wrong
  result rather than a late error.
- **By a VB6-shaped error** (a numbered run-time error, a compile error, or a "Run-time error" with no
  number). These were already shown. The ones with no number can still be trapped, and under
  `On Error Resume Next` they are skipped with `Err.Number` left at 0. That is Silently wrong under an
  error handler, and it is tracked in [#257](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/257).

**Not every absence is safe to demote into No-op.** `DoEvents` returning 0 without pumping messages, as it
did until #766, withheld a behaviour and lied about nothing. `Print #` as a no-op would silently not write the user's
file — the same harm shape as a silently lossy save, which
[`serialization-outcomes.md`](serialization-outcomes.md) rules out entirely. So the question for each gap
is not only *how expensive to close* but *can it be degraded honestly in the meantime*.

`Beep` is the cautionary tale in the other direction: it sat as a no-op justified by cross-platform
concerns until someone checked, and the justification was simply wrong — `Console.Beep()` is unsupported
only on platforms HexIDE does not ship to. It is now implemented. Before accepting a No-op row here,
check whether the reason still holds.

## Two things this document is not

**Nothing here is `vb6.exe`-verified.** The classification ran while the oracle VM was unreachable, so
every row is derived from reading HexIDE's own source, not from measuring real VB6. That is sound for
*does it run* — a `NotImplementedException` is a fact about our code — but it is **not** authority on
*does it behave correctly*. Where a Partial row describes a semantic difference, treat it as a lead to
verify, not a measurement. Verified behaviour only ever lives in
[`vb6-fidelity-oracle/README.md`](vb6-fidelity-oracle/README.md). A row re-rated since on the oracle's evidence names
the oracle rows it rests on, and says which parts no oracle row covers; for the type characters, the
suffix rows do.

**"Supported" is an optimistic ceiling.** Every row was attacked by an adversarial pass whose only job
was to disprove it. It returned a verdict on 323 originally-supported rows and **demoted 217 of them** —
**two thirds**. It did not reach all of them. So read Supported as *no one has yet shown this broken*,
and expect the real figure to be materially lower.

A worked example of what that means in practice: this document downgraded `Split` and `Filter` for
mishandling omitted optional arguments, and left `Join` as Supported — even though `Join` uses the *same*
`HasArg` helper, three lines away in the same file. The demotions are as incomplete as the attacks were.

**These statuses were derived by reading code, then corrected by execution where a verifier bothered to
run something.** Where a row says a construct throws, that is usually solid. Where it says something
*works*, treat it as a lead.

**The sweep is a snapshot, and work landed after it.** Rows overtaken since are marked inline with
**Superseded by #N**, rather than quietly rewritten, so it is visible which parts are second-hand. The
known case is [#184](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/pull/184), which made object member chains fold
on the *read* path — seven rows describe the single-dot restriction it removed. #822 (#1056) lifted it for
`obj.a.b = x` too, and it now survives only for `Set obj.a.b = o`.

## At a glance

| Category | Silently wrong | Won't load | Dies | Partial | No-op | Supported | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Statements | 1 | 0 | 9 | 50 | 0 | 47 | 107 |
| Operators | 0 | 0 | 1 | 20 | 0 | 11 | 32 |
| Intrinsic functions | 0 | 0 | 17 | 65 | 0 | 71 | 153 |
| Keywords and modifiers | 0 | 0 | 2 | 22 | 1 | 26 | 51 |
| Literals, types and suffixes | 0 | 1 | 2 | 20 | 0 | 27 | 50 |
| Compiler directives and options | 0 | 0 | 0 | 14 | 19 | 14 | 47 |
| In-box objects | 0 | 0 | 58 | 27 | 0 | 38 | 123 |
| Intrinsic constants | 0 | 0 | 0 | 258 | 0 | 363 | 621 |
| **Total** | **1** | **1** | **89** | **476** | **20** | **597** | **1184** |

The *Silently wrong* column counts only what a verifier proved by running it. It is a floor, not a
census — see the caveat above.


### The single most useful number here is 0

**No construct in this inventory fails to parse.** The last one, a `Function` declared with a type
character (`Function GetName$()`, [#740](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/740)), parses now. Every
gap parses cleanly and goes wrong at run time: mostly by throwing, sometimes by running on with a wrong
value. The one Silently wrong row below is a wrong value counted as such; the `()` row records
another under [#741](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/741) and stays Partial, for the reason
given there. The grammar comprehends essentially the whole of VB6; what is missing is execution.

It was four until numeric line labels were ported in from the LSP server's grammar, which already had
the rule — see below. It went back to four when the suffixed `Function` was measured, and that one has
the same shape: the LSP server's grammar accepts it and the interpreter's does not. It went to two when
conditional compilation took `#Const` off it ([#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)): the directives are applied to the module's text
before the parse, so the grammar never sees one, as vb6.exe's parser never sees an inactive branch. The `D` exponent
marker left it when vb6.exe was found to compile one ([#1611](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1611)), and the same measurement found a
literal ending in its point, which took its place. That went to one when both grammars learned to read it
([#1615](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1615)), and to none when the interpreter's `functionStmt` took the
`typeHint?` the LSP server's grammar had (#740).

That matters for three reasons. It is the empirical form of the claim in `CLAUDE.md` that no VB6
construct is incomprehensible to the CST, which until now was an architectural assertion rather than a
measurement. It means a user opening real VB6 source will nearly always *see* their code, correctly
highlighted, with working navigation, even where it will not run — a far better first impression than a
file that refuses to open. And it means the remaining work is almost entirely additive: implementing a
visitor, not changing how the language is understood.

### Where the damage actually is

**Operators are nearly complete** — 1 absent of 32 — so expressions and arithmetic generally work. The
damage is concentrated in **statements** and the **in-box object model**: 8% of VB6's statements (9
of 107) and 47% of the
in-box surface (`App`, `Printer`, `Clipboard`; 58 of 123) are unavailable. Both
figures are rounded to the nearest whole percent.

That is the honest answer to "will my code run?" — the arithmetic will; the plumbing around it often will
not. Text files run since [#1145](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1145): `Open` For Input, Output and
Append, `Line Input #`, `Print #`, `Close`, `FreeFile` and `EOF`; `Input #` and `Write #` since
[#1521](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1521); `Kill`, `Dir` and the other filesystem statements since [#1561](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1561). Still absent as a block:
`LOF`, `Loc`, `Seek` and the `Input` function run since
[#1645](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1645), and Random and Binary files, `Get` and `Put` since [#1646](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1646).

## Silently wrong — 1 name

**The six type characters used to be here ([#739](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/739)).** An
assignment through one, `s$ = "x"`, wrote a second variable named `s$`, and a `For i%` counter ran on one. Both
now reach the variable of the bare name, checked against its declared type, as the oracle's 2026-10-11
assignment probes and its 2026-10-27 counter probes measured, and an undeclared name takes the character's type.
A read through one runs too since [#742](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/742), checked the same way, so the six rows have left Dies for Partial.

**Measured, and recorded under [#741](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/741) rather than here:
call resolution.** A parameterless `Function Left() As String` called as `Left("abc", 1)` returns the
program's own value here, where VB6 compiles the call and returns the library's `"a"` (TE_01). The same
function without an `As` clause returns its value here with no error, where VB6 compiles the call and
raises error 13 at run time (TE_04). TE_01 is a wrong result from a program VB6 runs; TE_04 is a missing
run-time error, not a wrong result. TE_01 needs a parameterless procedure that shares a library
function's name and is called with that function's arguments, since the library supplies VB6's answer;
whether TE_04's needs the name too is open (see the `F` bullet below). Rating the call operator itself Silently wrong for
TE_01 would misdescribe the common case, which resolves correctly, so it is recorded under #741 and the
`() (call / array index)` row stays Partial and carries the link. Two neighbours in the same issue, both
missing errors rather than wrong results:

- `Dim Left As String` followed by `Left("abc", 1)` reaches the library's `Left` here, where VB6 refuses
  to compile it with "Expected array" (TL_07; TF_04 for a module variable).
- Surplus arguments are dropped, where VB6 refuses to compile the call. `H(5, 6)` on
  `Function H(ByVal a As Long) As Long` gives 5 here, and `G(5, 6)` on a parameterless
  `Function G() As Long` gives 1; for both, VB6 gives the compile error "Wrong number of arguments or
  invalid property assignment". `F(5, 6)` on a parameterless `Function F()` with no `As` clause gives 1
  here, and VB6 gives the compile error "Expected array", where TE_04, the same shape named `Left`,
  compiles and raises error 13; `F` ran in the scripted harness and TE_04 in a project, so whether the
  name or the harness makes that difference is open. None of the three raises anything here. (Oracle: the 2026-10-11
  entry "A type character on a name the program also declares", the surplus-argument rows beside TE_01–TE_04.)

**Closed earlier by [#191](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/191).** Eight constructs used to
sit here: `Shell`, `FreeFile`, `Command`, `CurDir`, `Cls`, `Erl`, `Dir` and `Time = x`. Each parsed, ran,
did nothing, and let the program continue — so the user saw no error and went looking for a bug in their
own code.

Two causes, both now fixed. An unregistered bare name fell through to VB6's implicit-declaration rule and
evaluated to `Empty`; the read path now raises for any name VB6 defines as an intrinsic, and the
bare-statement path no longer discards the "not a builtin" result. And `Time = x` was grammar-shadowed —
`TIME` is an ambiguousKeyword and `letStmt` was matched ahead of `timeStmt`, so it silently created a
*variable* called `Time` and left the throw in `VisitTimeStmt` as unreachable dead code. `dateStmt`
already sat ahead of `letStmt`, which is the only reason `Date = x` threw and `Time = x` did not.

**The category stayed in this document at zero**, because it is the failure mode worth watching for:
anything that fails without saying so belongs here rather than under Dies, and the reasoning above is how
to recognise the next one. The check keys off a list of VB6's *surface* rather than of our gaps, so
implementing a function does not require maintaining it. It could not have caught the type characters,
which are the program's own names spelled with a suffix rather than names VB6 defines.

**A standard module's Property Get is not reached by its name ([#977](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/977)).** `x = PG`, with
`Property Get PG() As Long()` in a standard module, reads Empty here with no error, where VB6 reads the array; the name
is taken for an undeclared variable. Found while fixing #975. A class's Property Get is unaffected.

| Name | Status | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Property Get` | **Silently wrong** | **#79, phase 1:** storage allocated by the call is reclaimed on return and error unwind. **Re-rated from Partial by measurement ([#977](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/977)).** A standard module's Property Get is not reached by its name: `x = PG`, with `Property Get PG() As Long()` in a module, reads Empty here with no error, where VB6 reads the array (measured), and `PG()` is "Sub or Function not defined". A class's is unaffected, and one declared `As Long()` returns its array ([#975](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/975), measured). **[#1444](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1444):** a return declared as a scalar converts what is stored into its name there and then, as a variable of that type does, and a failure is raised inside the procedure, where its own handler traps it (measured: `FS = 5` As String is "5", `FD = "0.5"` As Date 00:05, `FB = "abc"` As Boolean 13, and `FI = 40000` As Integer 6, inside each). Function-like: returns via its own name, dispatched on a member READ of a class instance. A `As <Class>` return carries the concrete name so the return slot seeds a real UDT or Nothing… | PrePass.cs, ExpressionExecutor.cs |
## Won't load — 1 name
One construct is on the list (#2529). A parse failure takes the entire module down — nothing in the file runs and the editor
**`Function` declared with a type character used to be here** ([#740](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/740)). `Function GetName$()` did not parse, so its module never loaded: the interpreter's `functionStmt` had no `typeHint?` after the name, though the LSP server's had one. All six characters were measured on vb6.exe, native and p-code, and the rule ported across. Both rows are Partial again, as they were before the character was measured, since the body may assign the return through it too ([#739](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/739)).
**A literal ending in its point, and the `D` exponent, used to be here.** vb6.exe compiles `5.` and `5.D1`, and both
([#1611](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1611), [#1615](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1615)).
**Multiple file numbers on one line used to fail here** ([#1146](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1146)).
The date-literal token swallowed the text between two `#`s, so `Line Input #1, s: Print #2, s` and
`Close #1, #2` prevented a whole module from parsing. Both grammars now delimit date/time components instead of
arbitrary text. Real dates still win where VB6 gives them that meaning: `#1, 2#` is a date, even after `Print`.
`10 Debug.Print 1` with no syntax error and the module then refused to load. HexIDE's two halves
disagreed about what VB6 is. `GrammarParityTests` now compares the two rule inventories so the next such
which is the cheaper kind to close. It runs since [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112), with `#Const`, and the named-argument
operator `:=`, the other such gap, since [#1465](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1465).
| Name | Status | Detail | Source |
| `Line number` | Won't load | **[#2529](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/2529):** a number on a closing or middle line of a block, `30 Next`, `End If`, `Else`, `ElseIf`, `Loop`, `Wend`, a later `Case`, `End Select`, `End With` or `End Sub`, is a syntax error, so the module doesn't load. vb6.exe accepts every one, and refuses only a number between `Select Case` and its first `Case` (measured). Tools that number lines for `Erl` number these too. On a statement a number parses, and works as a `GoTo`, `On Error GoTo` and `Resume` target. | VB6.g4, VisualBasic6Parser.g4 |


Nothing is on the list now. A parse failure takes the entire module down — nothing in the file runs and the editor
cannot open it usefully — so however obscure each construct below looked, it had a blast radius of a file rather
than a statement. That is why each was fixed ahead of far more commonly used constructs that merely throw.


grammars needed a digit after the point and lexed no `D` in a whole number. Both were measured and are read now


**Numeric line labels used to be here**, and how they were found is the useful part: the LSP server's
grammar already had a working `lineNumber` rule that the interpreter's lacked, so the *editor* accepted
gap is a build failure rather than a bug report. It compares inventories, though, not what each rule
accepts, which is how the suffixed `Function` got past it: both grammars have a `functionStmt`, and only
the LSP server's lets a type character follow the name.

Worth noting what is *not* here: `#If` parsed fine and threw, an execution gap rather than a grammar gap,

|---|---|---|---|

are visible — and they are very clustered: the file-I/O family beyond text files is absent as a block, and so is
most of the in-box object model.

## Full inventory by category

Every name, including the ones that work. Sorted within each table by F5 impact — Silently wrong, Won't
load, Dies, Partial, No-op, Supported — because a reader of a coverage document is looking for what is
missing.


### Statements — 107 names (1 silently wrong, 9 absent, 50 partial, 47 supported)

| Name | Status | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `On Error Resume Next` | Supported | **#767:** an unimplemented fault suppresses Class_Terminate during reference cleanup, so a raising termination hook cannot replace it with a trappable error.  **#839:** a block statement whose condition or header raises goes on into the block, as VB6 does: `If k Then` runs the Then branch, `ElseIf k` its branch, a one-line `If k` its Then part, and `Do While k`, `Do Until k` and `While k` run their bodies, asking again each time round, where the whole statement was skipped (measured by #840 and #839, native and in the IDE). Where native code and p-code differ, for a Select Case selector and a For loop's start, limit or step, HexIDE follows p-code, which is how the IDE runs a program. On Error GoTo with Resume Next in the handler still resumes after the whole top-level statement (see `Resume Next`) — Sets errorMode = ErrorMode.ResumeNext, trapped per statement in every nested block (VisitBlock) and at the top-level pc driver. **#877:** it clears Err, as every On Error does, and while a handler is running it traps nothing: an error inside the handler goes to the caller, as vb6.exe sends it (measured). | StatementExecutor.cs |
| `Function` | Partial | **#2120:** procedure calls keep their own bindings and fall back to the module or instance, avoiding a copy of every module name on each call; name lookup and the Locals display are preserved. **#79, phase 1:** activation slots are freed on return and error unwind; ByRef aliases and persistent Static slots are retained. **A type character declares the return ([#740](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/740), measured for all six).** `Function F%()` returns an Integer, and `& ! # @ $` a Long, Single, Double, Currency and String, ahead of a DefType for the name's letter. A call may carry the character, `F%()` or `F% + 1`, and it is checked against the return type: another character, or one on a Variant Function, is VB6's compile error "Type-declaration character does not match declared data type". A character and an `As` clause together are "Expected: end of statement". The body may assign the return through the character, `F% = 7`, as VB6 does ([#739](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/739)). **[#1444](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1444):** a return declared as a scalar converts what is stored into its name there and then, as a variable of that type does, and a failure is raised inside the procedure, where its own handler traps it (measured: `FS = 5` As String is "5", `FD = "0.5"` As Date 00:05, `FB = "abc"` As Boolean 13, and `FI = 40000` As Integer 6, inside each). Otherwise as before — PrePass.VisitFunctionStmt registers a ProcedureInfo (name, params, return type, body, IsPrivate). The return is by assignment to the procedure's own name; an unassigned return yields the… | VB6.g4, PrePass.cs, BasicInterpreter.cs |
| `Function...End Function` | Partial | **#79, phase 1:** activation slots are freed on return and error unwind; ByRef aliases and persistent Static slots are retained. **A type character declares the return ([#740](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/740)); the body may assign it through the character ([#739](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/739)); see `Function`.** **[#975](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/975):** one declared `As Long()` returns its array (measured), where it returned a Long 0, and **[#986](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/986):** takes only an array of its own type, a mismatch raising 13 or VB6's compile error "Can't assign to array" as VB6 does (measured), and a String stored into one declared `As Byte()`, which VB6 converts, stops the run as not implemented ([#998](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/998)). The production quoted below now has `typeHint?` after the name (#740). Otherwise as before — The whole construct - `(visibility WS)? (STATIC WS)? FUNCTION WS name (argList)? (asTypeClause)? ... END_FUNCTION` - is collected by PrePass.VisitFunctionStmt and executed by… | VB6.g4, PrePass.cs, BasicInterpreter.cs |

| `DefBool` | Supported | **#169:** types every declaration with no As clause by the first letter of its name, as vb6.exe does (measured): an undeclared variable, a `Dim` or `Static`, a field, a parameter and a Function's return. A `Const` keeps its value's type. An overlap, a DefType after a declaration or inside a procedure, and a name longer than a letter are VB6's compile errors. Not yet: a type character on such a variable ([#742](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/742)) and a ReDim that declares ([#1495](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1495)). | PrePass.cs |

| `End` | Supported | Ends the program where it stands, as VB6 does (measured, #775): nothing after it runs, no form raises QueryUnload, Unload or Terminate, and no Class_Terminate runs. In the IDE it ends its own run as the End command does and reports nothing; an old or unrelated interpreter's End cannot end the current run (#761). Headless, the interpreter stops and starts nothing again. | StatementExecutor.cs, BasicInterpreter.cs |

| `Get` | Partial | **[#1646](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1646):** reads a Binary file's bytes or a Random file's record into a variable, an array element or a Type's field, in the bytes vb6.exe wrote: each scalar type, a String with its length except alone in a Binary file, a Variant by its type, arrays first index fastest with a descriptor for a dynamic array in a Random file, Types packed. Zeros past a Binary file's end, 59 past a Random record, EOF once a read runs past the end, and after it the next `Get` or `Put` with no position at the end, all as measured. **Partial:** refused where HexIDE can't hold the value (a Boolean of other bits, a Date past 9999, a Decimal of scale past 28, an Error Variant), and for arrays of a Type and Types containing array fields (still refused after [#178](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/178) enables runtime allocation), and fixed-length strings. | StatementExecutor.Records.cs, VbRecordFormat.cs, VbFiles.Records.cs |
| `GoSub...Return` | **Dies** | throws `"GoSub not implemented"` — Both halves parse and both throw. GoSub: (StatementExecutor.cs). Return: `throw new NotImplementedException("Return not implemented")`… | StatementExecutor.cs |
| `Input #` | Partial | **[#1521](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1521):** each variable takes the next field, read for its type as vb6.exe reads it. A numeric variable reads a number, which a blank ends, and 0 where the field starts with none; a String reads the text to the comma; a Variant is typed by what the field holds (Integer, Long, Currency up to 4 places, Decimal beyond, Double for an exponent, `#TRUE#`, `#NULL#`, a `#date#`). A point is a point under any locale. At the end of the file it is 62, and a Variant is emptied first. It reads a Binary file too, where a Ctrl+Z is an ordinary byte, and on a Random file is 54 ([#1646](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1646)). **Partial:** only a plain variable is taken, as for `Line Input #`, and `#ERROR n#` into a Variant remains unimplemented after #2520 added Error values. | StatementExecutor.cs, VbFiles.cs, VbInput.cs |
| `Kill` | Partial | Filesystem operations implemented against compiled VB6 measurements (#1561); host attributes, long-name wildcard matching and interpreter-local current directory: see fidelity oracle. | VbFiles.FileSystem.cs |
| `Line Input #` | Partial | **[#1145](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1145):** reads a line of an Input file in the ANSI code page, ending at CR or CR LF but not a lone LF, and stopping at Ctrl+Z; 62 past the end, with the variable unchanged (all measured). It reads a Binary file too, where a Ctrl+Z is an ordinary byte, and on a Random file is 54 ([#1646](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1646)). Into a plain variable only: an array element or a member is refused as not implemented. | StatementExecutor.cs, VbFiles.cs |
| `Lock` | Partial | **[#2553](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/2553):** whole-file, single-record, inclusive `start To end` and omitted-start `To end` ranges run. Binary positions are bytes; Random positions are records of `Len`; sequential ranges cover the whole file. Duplicate locks and non-exact unlocks raise 70, invalid ranges 63, unopened numbers 52; Close releases the handle’s locks. Other numbers’ Get/Put and sequential Line Input respect locks; the owner can read its own range. **Partial:** range conversions from types other than Byte, Integer or Long are unmeasured and refused as not implemented. Locks hold within this program only; other processes and Binary text reads are not covered by these measurements. | StatementExecutor.cs, VbFiles.Locks.cs |

Fixed-length String declarations, UDT copying, other target types, other source conversions and member/indexed targets remain not implemented. | StatementExecutor.cs |

| `Open` | Partial | **[#1145](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1145):** For Input, Output and Append run as measured: Output truncates, a missing Input file is 53, a number in use or a second Output of one file is 55, `""` is 75 and `#0` is 52. `Access` is taken; a `Lock` keeps the file from this program only. For Random and For Binary run since [#1646](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1646): a missing file is made, even with `Access Read`; a second handle on a file is 55 unless both are Input or both Binary or Random, which the `Lock` and `Access` clauses then decide between as Windows does, 70 where they clash; with no `Access` clause a handle takes reading and writing, then writing, then reading, all as measured. | StatementExecutor.cs, VbFiles.cs |
| `Preserve` | Supported | **#178:** growing or shrinking a UDT array retains every field of surviving records, including dynamic-array fields, and initializes new elements independently (measured). **[#1459](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1459):** `ReDim Preserve` keeps the elements still in range and changes only the last dimension's upper bound, as vb6.exe does (measured, native and p-code): a grow keeps every value and fills the new elements with the type's default, a shrink drops the elements past the new bound and terminates an object held only there, and any other bound changing, the number of dimensions included, is 9 and leaves the array as it was. Too big is 7 and leaves it as it was, where a plain ReDim erases it. A Variant holding an array keeps its type; an Empty Variant, or one holding a scalar, is 13. An `As` clause naming another type is the compile error "Can't change data types of array elements", or 13 on a Variant. A fixed array named directly is "Array already dimensioned", 10 through a parameter, and an array whose element a running call holds ByRef is 10 (#1455). An undimensioned dynamic array is dimensioned, as a plain ReDim does it. | StatementExecutor.cs, Vb6Value.cs |
| `Print` | Partial | **#993:** parses with an optional output list, so an unreached statement no longer prevents form loading. In standard and class modules it stops with a not-implemented refusal naming #993; VB6 behaviour there is unmeasured. | StatementExecutor.cs |

| `Const` | Partial | **#1073:** an untyped `Const C = -32768` holds a Long; `Const C = -2 ^ 2` holds Double -4. `Const C As Integer = -32768` still converts to Integer. `Const C = -&H8000` is refused with Overflow at run time rather than VB6's compile time. **[#1622](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1622), [#1499](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1499), fixed:** a name declared twice is vb6.exe's compile error, refused as the module loads: "Ambiguous name detected" at module level, "Duplicate declaration in current scope" in a procedure, parameters included (measured). It ran, keeping one declaration. **[#1614](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1614), fixed:** a Const declared with a type character, `Const K% = 2.5`, takes the character as the As clause it stands for, as vb6.exe's does: the Integer 2, `Const KS! = 2.5D2` the Single 250, and `Const K% = 40000` "Overflow" (measured). It stopped as the module loaded. A second declaration of a name at module level, with or without a character, is refused as vb6.exe refuses it ([#1622](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1622)). **#1003:** a store into a Const is refused, with VB6's message for the statement (measured): `MK = 2` and `Set MK = Nothing` are "Assignment to constant not permitted", a For counter, a For Each variable or a Mid target is "Variable required - can't assign to this expression", and `ReDim MK(2)` or `Erase MK` is "Expected array", raised as the statement runs. It used to overwrite the constant. **#760:** a procedure's Consts are declared at its entry, ahead of its Dims, so `Dim a(P)` sees `P` (measured: `Const P = 2.5` then `Dim a(P)` is `a(0 To 2)`). **#894, fixed:** a procedure Const or Dim bound reading a later local Const is refused ("Variable not defined" under Option Explicit, "Constant expression required" without, measured). Under Option Explicit, an earlier Const reading a module Const before a local Const of that name is "Duplicate declaration in current scope"; shadowing without the earlier read still works. **#873:** a Const's `As` type converts its value as a store into that type does, at module and procedure level (`As Integer = 2.5` is 2, `As String = 5` "5", `As Date = 1` 31/12/1899), and a value the type cannot hold is VB6's compile error ("Overflow" for `As Byte = 300`, "Type mismatch" for `As Integer = "x"`, "Invalid use of Null" for `As Integer = Null`), where the As clause was ignored; a String given to `As Date` is read as `CDate` reads it, and one that is no date is "Type mismatch", a serial beyond the range included ([#967](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/967), measured); Currency from a Double rounds its exact binary value to four places, ties to even (#966). A module-level Const is checked against VB6's rules for a constant expression as it runs, before it is evaluated (all measured with `/make`): a Const or Enum below, the Const itself, a variable, a user function and any function call, `Len` included, are "Constant expression required"; a constant qualified by its library, container, module, Enum or the project is read (`VBA.vbCrLf`, `KeyCodeConstants.vbKeyA`, `Module2.Cols.colMax`), and another module's declaration is found before a built-in constant of the same name; another module's Public Const is read, bare ([#880](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/880)) or qualified, initialising that module's declarations on demand first ([#893](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/893), fixed); re-entering a module still initialising is "Circular dependencies between modules", including a cycle through an array bound; and the module naming itself is "Circular dependencies between modules". **#1893:** the module-level constant-expression validation walk probes available host stack space and raises Error 28 before a long chain can overflow, including on a 1 MiB stack. Long-source VB6 acceptance remains unmeasured. Other procedure Const-expression checks remain unchanged — Module-level names are hoisted by PrePass (so a Sub declared before the Const can still see it) and filled by the runtime visitor; local Consts inside a Sub work; multiple per line work… **#1006 fixed:** `Module1.MK = 2` reaches the Const store guard and is refused with "Assignment to constant not permitted", as measured during #1003 and recorded in #1006; it raised 424. | StatementExecutor.cs, PrePass.cs |

Whole-record stores copy every field deeply; ReDim Preserve retains survivors, ReDim resets, Erase resets a fixed array or frees a dynamic one, and a ByRef element locks its containing array (10), all measured native and p-code. Indexed field access retains its subscripts; whole-array field stores use ArrayStore. Nonliteral or inverted Type-field bounds, fixed-length strings and array fields As New remain refused. **#894, fixed:** a bound reading a later procedure Const is refused with "Variable not defined" under Option Explicit and "Constant expression required" without (measured), instead of sizing from the hoisted Const. **[#1622](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1622), [#1499](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1499), fixed:** a name declared twice is vb6.exe's compile error, refused as the module loads: "Ambiguous name detected" at module level, "Duplicate declaration in current scope" in a procedure, parameters included (measured). It ran, keeping one declaration. **#760, #849:** a bound is coerced as `CLng` coerces it (banker's rounding, measured for Dim; Null 94, a non-numeric string 13 and beyond a Long 6 were measured for ReDim). A module-level Dim bounded by anything but a plain integer loads, where it failed to load the module, and its bounds are read where it is allocated, by VB6's rules, all measured with `/make`: a same-module Const above it is read, each module-level Const being checked itself as it runs (see `Const`); a same-module Enum member, bare or as `Sizes.eMax`, another module's Enum member qualified by its Enum, and a built-in constant are read; `Abs`, `CBool`, `CByte`, `CCur`, `CDbl`, `CInt`, `CLng`, `CSng`, `CStr`, `Fix`, `Int`, `Len` and `Sgn` of constants are called, `Len` only of a String, `"ab" + "c"` included ("Variable required" of a number, and of `"ab" + 1`) and none of them through a qualifier (`VBA.Len("ab")` is "Constant expression required"); a dimension with no values (`Dim d(-1)`, `Dim d(1 To N)` with N 0, `Dim d(5 To 2)`) is "Range has no values", in a module, a class or a procedure, where it built an array with no elements or raised 9; a Const below, a variable, a user function, a function call in a Const, another module's Public or Global variable and 22 other built-ins (`Asc`, `IIf`, `Rnd`, `Val`...) are "Constant expression required"; a name the module cannot see is "Variable not defined" under Option Explicit; the module's own name as a qualifier is "Circular dependencies between modules"; another module's Public Const is read, bare ([#880](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/880)) or qualified, initialising its module on demand first ([#893](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/893), fixed); a cycle through a bound is "Circular dependencies between modules"; another module's Public Enum member is read bare, a class's included ([#889](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/889)); and a built-in not yet measured there is refused; and on a form's run such an array is sized too, since its declarations run into its instance before `Form_Initialize` ([#822](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/822); it was refused, [#256](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/256)) — **[#2409](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/2409), fixed:** `Dim n$`, `Private s$`, `Static i%` and `Dim a%(3)` declare the character's type, as the As clause it stands for would, at module level and locally, and so does a parameter's, `Sub F(n%)` (measured); with an As clause as well, each is VB6's compile error. — Dim is treated as a DECLARATION, not an executable statement: HoistDeclaredLocals allocates every local a procedure declares before the body runs, in declaration order, and DeclareLocal is… **#965 fixed:** `Module2.g(1)` reads the Public array's element (5, a Long), rather than the whole Long() array, as measured on vb6.exe. | StatementExecutor.cs, PrePass.cs |
| `Enum` | Partial | **#1107:** known referenced managed libraries share the control-library enum model, with bare, enum-qualified and library-qualified members.  **#1637, fixed (SP6, native and p-code measured):** when an Enum and its member share a name, `E.E` reads the Long member, in a standard module, a Private class Enum and across modules (`Module2.E.E` too). ### Operators — 32 names (1 absent, 20 partial, 11 supported)

**Precedence ([#1074](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1074)).** Until #1074 each operator had a precedence level of its own, so `10 - 2 + 3` was 5 and `5 * 5 \ 2` was 10, with no error. Operators now group as VB6 groups them (measured): `*` and `/` share a level, then `\`, then `Mod`, then `+` and `-`, then every comparison, `Like` and `Is` included, each level left to right. A Boolean beside a number now compares as that number, True as -1. **#1073:** `-2 ^ 2` now gives Double -4: the sign is parsed as negation after the power, and `x-1` subtracts without spaces. The rows below are about each operator's own semantics.

| Name | Status | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|

Works on a Collection ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)): `c!key` is `c("key")`, `c![a key]` brackets a key with spaces, and `!key` works inside `With c`. `c!key = 1` is 424, as in VB6. **#2066:** a form also accepts bang reads: `Me!Name` reaches the same control as `Controls!Name` and `Controls("Name")`, through its default Controls member (measured for #1556, native and p-code). Other objects still stop the run as not implemented. | ExpressionExecutor.cs |

A form's `Print` method is not implemented, so neither is `Tab` in it. | VbPrint.cs |

This works in a call to the program's own Sub, Function or class method, and to the 73 intrinsics VB6 takes names for, with VB6's compile errors raised as the statement runs (measured: an unknown name is "Named argument not found", one given twice "Named argument already specified", a positional argument after a named one "Syntax error", and a ParamArray "Argument in ParamArray may not be named"). **Named arguments are evaluated in parameter order, not the order written** (measured: `Foo c:=F("c"), a:=F("a")` evaluates `a` first, and `Mid(Start:=…, String:=…)` evaluates String first). Twenty intrinsics refuse every name, as VB6 does ("Syntax error"): Abs, the `C…` conversions but CDec and CVDate, CVErr, Fix, InStr, Int, LBound, Len, Sgn, StrComp and UBound. The six with no parameters refuse a name as VB6 does, three ways ([#1475](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1475): Command, Now and Time "Named arguments not allowed", Date "Syntax error", DoEvents and Timer "Wrong number of arguments or invalid property assignment"). `RaiseEvent` refuses them too. The ParamArray form `T a()` arrives as one element holding a copy, leaving the caller unchanged on writes through that element (#2532; measured native and p-code in #2474). **#849:** an array subscript is coerced as `CLng` coerces it (measured: banker's rounding, a numeric string, True -1, Null 94, "x" 13, beyond a Long 6, and an omitted `Optional` 13), and a control-array index as `CInt` (6 outside an Integer, 341 for a negative index, 13 for an omitted `Optional`), where only an Integer was accepted. **Kept Partial on purpose; a wrong result recorded under [#741](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/741).** A parameterless `Function Left() As String` called as `Left("abc", 1)` returns the program's own value, where VB6 returns the library's `"a"` (oracle TE_01): a wrong result from a program VB6 runs. The same function without an `As` clause returns its value with no error, where VB6 raises error 13 at run time (TE_04): a missing run-time error, not a wrong result. TE_01 needs a parameterless procedure that shares a library function's name and is called with that function's arguments, so it is recorded under #741 rather than rating the call operator Silently wrong, which would misdescribe the common case. Missing compile errors besides: `Dim Left As String` then `Left("abc", 1)` reaches the library where VB6 refuses to compile it with "Expected array" (TL_07, TF_04); and surplus arguments are dropped with no error, `H(5, 6)` on `Function H(ByVal a As Long) As Long` giving 5 and `G(5, 6)` on a parameterless `Function G() As Long` giving 1, where VB6 gives "Wrong number of arguments or invalid property assignment", and `F(5, 6)` on a parameterless `Function F()` with no `As` clause giving 1, where VB6 gives "Expected array" (the oracle's surplus-argument rows beside TE_01–TE_04; whether TE_04 differs from `F` by name or by harness is open there). | `Line label` | Partial | **#223:** falling through `Fin: End If` now runs; jumps to terminator labels remain deferred. **Fixed** — a label is now a `lineHead` on the block, reachable only after a separator containing a real line break, so a label SHARING its line with a statement registers as a jump target. Ten of fourteen measured-legal forms used to be silently lost (`Skip: stmt`, `Later : stmt`, `Skip:: stmt`, an On Error handler target, a label split from its colon by a continuation, …), because `lineLabel` was a `blockStmt` competing for the colon the block needed as a separator. A label named for a keyword (`Error:`, `Name:`) now parses too. Remaining gap: a label sharing its line with a construct TERMINATOR (`Cont: Next i`, `Fin: End If`) parses but is not a jump target — that is the tree-walking limit, not this defect; see interpreter-gaps.md | VB6.g4, StatementExecutor.cs |
| `LineContinuation` | Partial | **#224:** supplies visible whitespace between word fragments, absorbing indentation, so `val _` / `ue = 5` no longer assigns to `value` (the measured probe prints Long 0). Multi-word keywords still accept continuations. Spaces or tabs are required before the underscore; only spaces or tabs may follow it before CRLF, LF or CR ([#1426](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1426), measured). A line holding only a continuation is one line boundary, which `NEWLINE` takes whole. A comment ending in ` _` continues past any of the three. **#1223 measurement:** continuation-only stress inputs complete through 100,000 sequences; long operator chains now raise trappable Error 28 rather than overflow the runtime walk (#1893). **#1894:** LSP visitor traversal is now bounded; interpreter expression traversal remains unchanged. ### Compiler directives and options — 47 names (0 absent, 14 partial, 19 no-op, 14 supported)

| Name | Status | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| `#Const` | Partial | **Runs since [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112).** Applied to the module's text before the parse, as `#If` is. It holds from its line to the end of its module, across procedures; it defines its constant even inside an inactive branch, and a second one for the name anywhere in the module is "Duplicate definition" (both measured). It may shadow a project argument or a built-in. **#1893:** expression trees deeper than 300 nodes take the existing not-an-expression refusal path before evaluation. This is a host safety bound, not measured VB6 acceptance. Partial for `#If`'s reasons. Oracle: "Conditional compilation" | ConditionalCompiler.cs |
| `#Else` | Supported | **Runs since [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112).** A second `#Else` is vb6.exe's "An # Else clause must be preceded by an # If", and `#Else whatever` is not a directive (both measured) | ConditionalCompiler.cs |
| `#ElseIf` | Partial | As `#If` ([#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)). It is evaluated after a branch was taken too (measured: `#ElseIf 1 / 0` there is "Division by zero"), and after `#Else` it is vb6.exe's structure error | ConditionalCompiler.cs |
| `#End If` | Supported | **Runs since [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)**, as `#End If` or `#EndIf`. A stray one, and an `#If` never closed, are vb6.exe's compile errors | ConditionalCompiler.cs |
| `#If` | Partial | **Runs since [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112).** Each module's directives are applied to its text before the parse: an inactive branch is made empty, keeping the line numbers, so it is never parsed, as in vb6.exe, and may hold anything. Every condition is evaluated, an inactive branch's too, and an error is a compile error as the module loads (measured). A name is the module's `#Const`, the project's `CondComp`, VB6's `Win32` (1) or `Win16` (0), or a Variant holding 0. Strings compare as vb6.exe's text compare, whatever `Option Compare` says, and so does `Like`, with its error 93 ([#1598](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1598), measured). **#1893:** expression trees deeper than 300 nodes are refused before evaluation through the existing not-an-expression path, also for `#ElseIf`; long-chain VB6 acceptance remains unmeasured. Partial: every personality takes VB6's built-ins ([#1548](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1548)). Oracle: "Conditional compilation" | ConditionalCompiler.cs, ConstantExpression.cs |
| `Mac` | Supported | Not defined under VB6, so it reads 0 (measured, [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)) | CompilationConstants.cs |
| `VB_PredeclaredId` | Supported | A class header with True gives the class name a shared lazy default instance: first use initializes it, explicit New makes a separate object, Set to Nothing terminates it, and the next read (including Is Nothing) recreates it with reset fields. It lives until program shutdown. Measured native and p-code in [#2555](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/2555); PredeclaredClassTests pins the rows. | PrePass.cs, BasicInterpreter.cs, VBProgram.cs |
| `Vba6` | Partial | Not defined under VB6, so it reads 0 (measured, [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)). Partial because the VBA personalities read 0 too, where VBA defines it as 1 ([#1548](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1548)) | CompilationConstants.cs |
| `Win16` | Supported | 0 under VB6 (measured, [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)) | CompilationConstants.cs |
| `Win32` | Supported | 1 under VB6 (measured, [#1112](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1112)): `Win32 = 1` is true, `Win32 = True` false, and `Not Win32` true, since `Not` is bitwise | CompilationConstants.cs |
| `Attribute` | Partial | Parses everywhere VB6 writes it (attributeStmt, VB6.g4: a module header element and a block statement), and since #832 is accepted everywhere. A header's Attribute lines parse as moduleAttributes and are never run; since #273 every run gives the interpreter the whole file (FormCodeText.WholeFile), so that is the path every module's and form's header takes. A line inside a procedure or after a module-level declaration is metadata and runs as nothing, and the debugger treats it as a blank line: a step passes over it at any nesting and Set Next Statement onto it is refused. A breakpoint on a line of the header, or of a run that describes a member, is refused, because #273 makes those lines read-only in the code window; on any other Attribute line it is accepted and never reached, as on a blank line. Three attributes carry meaning HexIDE does not give them: VB_Name is read only by the serializer and the IDE; VB_UserMemId and VB_VarUserMemId declare a default member, which HexIDE collects but does not read yet, so a use of one as a value is refused naming #174. VB_PredeclaredId gives a class its lazy default instance since #2555. | StatementExecutor.cs, VBLoader.cs |

| `Collection` | Partial | **#1485, fixed:** `v = New Collection`, `v = MakeC()` and `v = MakeV()` empty a Variant before raising 450; a Collection held in a Collection, Object or Variant variable leaves the target alone (measured). **[#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471):** `Set c = New Collection` (or `New VBA.Collection`) makes one, and `Dim c As Collection` declares an object slot holding Nothing. TypeName is "Collection", VarType 9, `TypeOf c Is Collection` True. Items are held by reference and released in order when the last reference goes. For Each walks it with a live cursor, as VB6 does: removing the current item goes on to the next. `c!key` and `c![a key]` are `c("key")`. Errors match vb6.exe (5, 9, 13, 91, 424, 438, 448, 450, 451, 457), and so do the compile errors for too few or too many arguments. Late-bound through an Object or a Variant, those come at run time as 448, 450 or 451. `Dim c As New Collection` makes one on first use ([#435](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/435)). **#1482:** arrays `As Collection` now hold Nothing until Set, enforce Collection assignment, and release their contents on Erase. **Not yet:** keys compare ordinally ignoring case, so "straße" and "strasse" are two keys where VB6 calls them one ([#1479](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1479)). **#1567:** keys compare and hash as vb6.exe's text compare does, so `"strasse"` finds `"straße"` (measured, #1479). | VbCollection.cs |
| `Collection._NewEnum` | **Dies** | For Each over a Collection works ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)), but the hidden member itself is not: a program that calls `c.[_NewEnum]` does not get an enumerator. | VbCollection.cs |
| `Collection.Add` | Supported | Item, Key, Before and After, by position or by name ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)). VB6's check order: Before and After both given (5), then the key (13, or 457 for a duplicate), then the position (9, or 5 for a missing key). A key that isn't a String is looked up as a position first, so one naming an existing item is 457 and anything else 13, as measured. | VbCollection.cs |
| `Collection.Count` | Supported | A Long ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)). `c.Count = 5` is VB6's compile error, and `o.Count(1)` late-bound is 451. | VbCollection.cs |
| `Collection.Item` | Supported | The default member, so `c(i)` and `c!key` reach it ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)). A String is a key, anything else a position rounded half to even: `c(1.5)` and `c(2.5)` are item 2. Empty and True are 9 and Null 13, and in an empty Collection every index is 5 ([#1492](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1492)). Assigning an item looks the index up first, then gives 424, or 451 or 438 late-bound. | VbCollection.cs |
| `Collection.Remove` | Supported | By position or key, also as `Index:=` ([#1471](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1471)). An object item is released when its last reference goes, and a For Each on the Collection goes on to the item after. | VbCollection.cs |

## Intrinsic constants — 621 names in 89 families

Constants are half the language surface by count and would swamp a table, so they are summarised by
family. They also have almost no nuance individually: a constant either resolves to the right value or it
does not.

**They resolve as VB6 resolves them.** In VB6 these are not flat constants: they are members of *enums*,
and of one *module*, published by the VBA and VB type libraries (`ColorConstants`, `VbMsgBoxStyle`,
`KeyCodeConstants`, `VbVarType`, `VBA.Constants` and so on), which is why the Object Browser was full of
them. Since [#215](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/215) HexIDE resolves each through its library, from a dump of the real type libraries: bare,
qualified by its library (`VBA.vbCancel`) or its container (`VbMsgBoxResult.vbCancel`) or both, and an
in-box enum is a type (`Dim x As VbMsgBoxResult`). `InBoxConstantTests` runs each form.

This section went on describing the flat name-to-value table #215 replaced, in which no enum type was
registered, for weeks after it stopped being true, and eight rows were rated Dies on that reading ([#1633](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1633)).

**The Object Browser still shows none of them.** Its VBA library is built solely from the LSP
`vb/builtinSymbols` response, which enumerates `VbSignatures.GetAll()` — a 97-entry list that contains no
constant. So it lacks not merely the *grouping* but every intrinsic constant.

The larger cause of Partial is a constant whose **consumer** is absent: a correct value that nothing can
use. Be careful reading these rows, though — the grading is inconsistent on exactly this point. The
raster-op family is graded Supported although `PaintPicture` does not exist, while the OLE and mouse-
pointer families are graded Partial for the same reason. The difference records which rows an adversarial
pass happened to attack, not a real distinction.

### Constants by name — 12

The family table counts every constant; these twelve are listed by name as well. Eleven are the members of
the `VBA.Constants` module, which belongs to no enum, so an inventory that walked only `TKIND_ENUM` would
miss the whole family, and they are the ones a reader comes looking for: `vbCrLf`, `vbNewLine`, `vbTab`.
The twelfth, `vbUseCompareOption`, is documented widely and declared nowhere.

Seven of the eleven were rated Dies here from before [#215](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/215) until [#1633](https://github.com/hexide-io/HexIDE/issues/1633), and so was `vbUseCompareOption`:
that made `vbNewLine` and `vbTab` read as the largest cliff in this inventory, when every one resolves.
`InBoxConstantTests` runs each of the eleven bare, qualified both ways and in a module-level `Const`.
